}\hss}}}}{}{}_{t}(\xi)+{\mathbf{v}}_{1}(t;{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi).

(5.16)

Let
{\mathbf{w}}^{0}\in\mathcal{H}^{1}
.
Then, the equation

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathbf{w}}(t)\\
&=S(t){\mathbf{w}}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}S(t-t^{\prime})\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\mathcal{Q}({\mathbf{u}}_{1},{\mathbf{w}}+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))(\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}+\pi_{1}S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))\end{pmatrix}dt^{\prime}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}S(t-t^{\prime})\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\big(\mathcal{Q}({\mathbf{u}}_{1},{\mathbf{w}}+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}\big)(\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}+\pi_{1}S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})})\end{pmatrix}dt^{\prime},\end{split}

(5.17)

where, for
C\geq 1
a universal constant,

\displaystyle\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})(t):=C^{-\frac{2}{\alpha}}(

\displaystyle 1+\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})\|_{X^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|\mathcal{Q}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t),{\mathbf{w}}(t)+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}})^{-\frac{4}{\alpha}},

(5.18)

is almost surely globally well-posed in
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and, moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{16}t}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+e^{-\frac{1}{16}t}\|{\mathbf{w}}^{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

(5.19)

Proof.

For simplicity, we introduce the shorthands
\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}}
for
\mathcal{Q}({\mathbf{u}}_{1},{\mathbf{w}}+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}:={\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}
.
Let
Y\subset C([0,+\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1})
be the Banach space

Y=\{{\mathbf{w}}\in C([0,+\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1}):\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{16}}\},\quad\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{Y}:=\sup_{t\geq 0}e^{\frac{t}{16}}\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

Define the operator
\Gamma:Y\to C([0,+\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1})
by

\displaystyle\Gamma[{\mathbf{w}}](t)

\displaystyle:=S(t){\mathbf{w}}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}S(t-t^{\prime})\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\mathcal{Q}({\mathbf{u}}_{1},{\mathbf{w}}+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))(\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}+\pi_{1}S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))\end{pmatrix}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}S(t-t^{\prime})\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\big(\mathcal{Q}({\mathbf{u}}_{1},{\mathbf{w}}+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}\big)(\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}+\pi_{1}S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})})\end{pmatrix}dt^{\prime}.

We want to show that if
C
is a big enough constant, then
\Gamma
is a contraction on
Y
with
\Lip(\Gamma)\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
We write

\displaystyle F({\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}}(\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}+\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0})-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}(\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}+\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}).

We want to estimate
F({\mathbf{w}})-F({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})
. We can assume without loss of generality that
\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})(t)\leq\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})(t)
. We have that

\displaystyle F({\mathbf{w}})-F({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~[(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}})-(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}})\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}](\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}+\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0})

(5.20)

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}](\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}+\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0})

(5.21)

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}](\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})

(5.22)

\displaystyle+[(\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}})\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}](\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}-\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})})

(5.23)

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})}](\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}-\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})})

(5.24)

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})}](\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})}-\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}).

(5.25)

First of all, notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}=(3\pi_{1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}+\pi_{1}({\mathbf{w}}+{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})+2\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0})\pi_{1}({\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}),

so

\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\lesssim(\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

(5.26)

Therefore, by definition of
\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})-\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})\|^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}(1+\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})

(5.27)

\displaystyle\times(1+\|{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}})^{-2}

\displaystyle\times(1+\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}})^{-1}

Therefore, by Lemma
5.5
, (
5.26
), and definition of
\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{wdiff1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}})-(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}})\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}\|_{L^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}+\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}}

(5.28)

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{16}}\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\|(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}})\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}(\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{Y}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})

\displaystyle\lesssim C^{-1}e^{-\frac{t}{8}}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y},

Similarly, recalling that
f\ast\rho_{\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{2}}=f\ast\rho_{\varepsilon_{1}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon_{2}}
and (
5.27
), we have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{wdiff2}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}\|_{L^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}+\pi_{1}S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}}

(5.29)

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{16}}(\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})-\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}))^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}(\|{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})

\displaystyle\lesssim C^{-1}e^{-\frac{t}{8}}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y}.

By Lemma
5.5
, we have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{wdiff3}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})}\|_{L^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}-\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}}

(5.30)

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{16}}(\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}))^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{16}}(\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}))^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y}

\displaystyle\lesssim C^{-1}e^{-\frac{t}{16}}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y},

Proceeding analogously,

\displaystyle\|\eqref{wdiff4}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{4}}\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}-\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}},

(5.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{5t}{16}}C^{-1}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y},

\displaystyle\|\eqref{wdiff5}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{4}}\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}(\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})-\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}))^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}

(5.32)

\displaystyle\lesssim C^{-1}e^{-\frac{t}{4}}(\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})-\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}))^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{5t}{16}}C^{-1}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y},

and finally,

\displaystyle\|\eqref{wdiff6}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{4}}(\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})-\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}}))^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}

(5.33)

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{5t}{16}}C^{-1}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y}.

By putting together (
5.28
), (
5.29
), (
5.30
), (
5.31
), (
5.32
), (
5.33
), we obtain

\|F({\mathbf{w}})-F({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{8}}C^{-1}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y},

so

\displaystyle\phantom{=\ }\|\Gamma[{\mathbf{w}}](t)-\Gamma[{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}](t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{t-t^{\prime}}{2}}\|F({\mathbf{w}})(t^{\prime})-F({\mathbf{w}}^{\prime})(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{t-t^{\prime}}{2}}e^{-\frac{t^{\prime}}{8}}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{8}}C^{-1}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y}.

Therefore, if
C
is big enough, we obtain that

\|\Gamma[{\mathbf{w}}]-\Gamma[{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}]\|_{Y}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}-{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|_{Y}.

In particular, by Banach’s fixed point theorem, we have that the equation (
5.17
) is globally well-posed in
\mathcal{H}^{1}
. Moreover, since
\Gamma
is a contraction with
\Lip(\Gamma)\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{Y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma[\mathbf{0}]\|_{Y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\mathbf{w}}^{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\sup_{t}e^{\frac{t}{16}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{t-t^{\prime}}{2}}\|F(\mathbf{0})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\mathbf{w}}^{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\sup_{t}e^{\frac{t}{16}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{t-t^{\prime}}{2}}e^{-\frac{t^{\prime}}{4}}\varepsilon(\mathbf{0})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{0}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\mathbf{w}}^{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}},

which proves (
5.19
).

∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
s>0
,
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\in X^{\alpha}
and
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
be the unique global solution of (
1.8
) with
{\mathbf{u}}|_{t=0}={\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}
and of the form (
5.16
). Let
{\mathbf{w}}
be the unique global solution to (
5.17
) with
{\mathbf{w}}^{0}=0
.
Then, the function

\displaystyle\begin{split}h(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\langle\nabla\rangle^{s}\big[&\mathcal{Q}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t),{\mathbf{w}}(t)+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})})\\
&\times(\pi_{1}{\mathbf{w}}(t)+\pi_{1}S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})\ast\rho_{\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})})\big],\end{split}

(5.34)

is adapted to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
. Moreover,
\|h(t)\|_{L^{2}}
is a continuous function of
t
, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|h\|_{L^{2}([0,\infty);L^{2}_{x})}^{2}\big]\leq C\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|^{2}_{X^{\alpha}}<+\infty,

(5.35)

where
C=C(\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}},\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})>0
is a nondecreasing function of its arguments.

Proof.

Since
{\mathbf{w}}
and
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
are adapted to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, it is easy to see from (
5.34
) that
h
is also adapted. Let

\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}=\mathcal{Q}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t),{\mathbf{w}}(t)+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}:={\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}
. By (
5.8
) with
\theta=s
, we see that
h(t)
is a continuous function of time with values in
L^{2}
and

\displaystyle\|h(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon({\mathbf{w}})^{-\frac{s}{2}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}}(\|{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|S(t)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})

(5.36)

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-\frac{t}{16}}(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}})^{k(s,\alpha)}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}},

where we used (
5.18
) and (
5.19
) in the last inequality, and
k(s,\alpha)
is an appropriate constant.
Therefore, by (
5.11
), (
5.19
) and (
3.10
),

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\ }\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{+\infty}\|h(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{t}{8}}(1+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{X^{\alpha}}+\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{w}}\|_{W^{\alpha,\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}})^{2k(s,\alpha)}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{2}dt\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{2}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{t}{8}}\Big[1+\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{2}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{2}+\eta(t;\|{\mathbf{u}}^{1}_{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})^{k(s,\alpha)}\Big]dt

\displaystyle\leq C(\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}},\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{2}.

∎

For
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\in X^{\alpha}
, we define

\displaystyle{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{h}:={\mathbf{w}}+{\mathbf{u}}_{1}+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}^{0}_{2}-{\mathbf{u}}^{0}_{1}),

where
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}(t)=\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0};\xi)
, and
{\mathbf{w}}
is the solution to (
5.17
) with
{\mathbf{w}}^{0}=\mathbf{0}
.
For
h
as in (
5.34
), comparing (
5.17
) with (
1.11
) we obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{h}=\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi+h)=\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi)+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})+{\mathbf{w}}(t).

(5.37)

Remark 5.9
.

Note that if
L_{t}
is the linear flow; namely,
L_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{0};\xi)=S(t){\mathbf{u}}_{0}+\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to4.0pt{\hss\hbox{
\hbox to3.4pt{\vbox to9.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.7pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}\hss}}}}{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to4.0pt{\hss\hbox{
\hbox to3.4pt{\vbox to9.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.7pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}{{{}}{\ignorespaces}{}{\ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.38 M 2.08 10.38 C 2.08 11.52 1.15 12.45 0 12.45 C -1.15 12.45 -2.08 11.52 -2.08 10.38 C -2.08 9.23 -1.15 8.3 0 8.3 C 1.15 8.3 2.08 9.23 2.08 10.38 Z M 0 10.38}{} \ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}\hss}}}}{}{}_{t}(\xi)
, then we can choose
h=0
and
{\mathbf{w}}=0
and obtain the analogous version of (
5.37
)

L_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi)=L_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0};\xi)+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}).

We then have for any
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
invariant,

\displaystyle\|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\|_{d_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbb{E}[d_{n}(S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}),{\bf{0}})]d\mu_{1}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})d\mu_{2}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})

\displaystyle\leq\int\big(ne^{-\frac{t}{4}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}\wedge 1\big)d\mu_{1}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})d\mu_{2}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})\to 0

as
t\to\infty
, which implies that
\|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\|_{d_{n}}=0
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular, we have that
\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}
, i.e. uniqueness of the invariant measure.

5.3.
Proof of Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.4

Given
n\in\mathbb{N}
, recall that

\displaystyle d_{n}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=1\wedge n\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|_{X^{\alpha}},

(5.38)

and denote by
\text{Lip}_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}
the set of
d_{n}
-Lipschitz continuous functions
F:X^{\alpha}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\|F\|_{d_{n}}\leq 1
and
\|F\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
R>0
be fixed. Given any
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\in B_{R}(\bf{0})
, there exist
t_{0}=t_{0}(n,R)>0
and
K(R)>0,K^{\prime}(R)>0
such that, for every
t\geq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi))\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big]\leq(1-K(R))\big(1\wedge K^{\prime}(R)\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big),

(5.39)

for any
F\in\textup{Lip}_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}
.

We postpone the proof of this proposition to the end of the section, and proceed to the proofs of Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.4
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Suppose, in order to obtain a contradiction, that there exist probability measures
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
,
\mu_{1}\neq\mu_{2}
, which are both invariant under the flow of (
1.8
). We first suppose that
\mu_{1}\perp\mu_{2}
. We will return to the case when they are not mutually singular at the end of the proof.
We fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
R>0
be large enough so that
\displaystyle{\min_{j=1,2}}\mu_{j,R}>\frac{1}{2}
, where
\mu_{j,R}:=\mu_{j}(B_{R}(\bf{0}))
and let
t=t(n,R)>0
and
K(R)>0
be given by Proposition
5.10
.
By Lemma
5.3
, Lemma
5.2

and the invariance of
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\|_{d_{n}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{F\in\text{Lip}_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg[\int_{X^{\alpha}}F({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})d\mu_{1}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})-\int_{X^{\alpha}}F({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})d\mu_{2}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sup_{F\in\text{Lip}_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}}\iintt_{X^{\alpha}\times X^{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi))\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big]d\mu_{1}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})d\mu_{2}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}).

Using that
F\in\text{Lip}_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}
and Proposition
5.10
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\|_{d_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{F\in\text{Lip}_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg|\iintt_{B_{R}({\bf{0}})\times B_{R}({\bf{0}})}\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi))\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big]d\mu_{1}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})d\mu_{2}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})\bigg|

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}+\mu_{1,R}(1-\mu_{2,R})+(1-\mu_{1,R})\mu_{2,R}+(1-\mu_{1,R})(1-\mu_{2,R})

\displaystyle\leq(1-K(R))\mu_{1,R}\mu_{2,R}+1-\mu_{1,R}\mu_{2,R}

\displaystyle=1-K(R)\mu_{1,R}\mu_{2,R}

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{1}{4}K(R).

Note that the right hand side of the above inequality is independent of
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Now, taking
n\to\infty
using Lemma
5.4
, we have shown

\displaystyle\|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq 1-\frac{1}{4}K(R)<1,

which is a contradiction to the fact that
\mu_{1}\perp\mu_{2}
. Thus,
\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}
.
If instead
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
are not mutually singular, then we consider the measures

\displaystyle\rho_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})_{+}(X^{\alpha})}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})_{+},

\displaystyle\rho_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\mu_{2}-\mu_{1})_{+}(X^{\alpha})}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{1})_{+}.

These are probability measures which are invariant under the flow of (
1.8
) and moreover,
\rho_{1}\perp\rho_{2}
. By the same argument as above with
(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
replacing
(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
we would obtain
\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{\text{TV}}<1
, which is a contradiction. Hence, we again conclude that
\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
, and let
\xi,\xi^{\prime}
be two independent copies of space-time white noise. Let
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\in X^{\alpha}
, and fix
R\gg 1
. We want to show that the stopping time

\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})=\min\{t:\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{0},\xi),\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi^{\prime})\in B_{R}(\mathbf{0}),\|\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{0},\xi)-\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi^{\prime})\|_{X^{\alpha}}>\varepsilon\}

is finite a.s.
Let
\nu_{t}=\nu_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})
be the law of the process

(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi),\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi^{\prime}))
, which takes values in
X^{\alpha}\times X^{\alpha}
.
By Theorem
1.1
and Proposition
4.3
, we have that

\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\nu_{t}dt\rightharpoonup\rho_{s}\otimes\rho_{s}

weakly.
For
R\gg 1
, let
f_{R,\varepsilon}:X^{\alpha}\times X^{\alpha}\to\mathbb{R}
be given by

f_{R,\varepsilon}(x,y)=\Big(1-1\wedge\frac{\|x-y\|_{X^{\alpha}}}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big(1-1\wedge\frac{\|x\|_{X^{\alpha}}}{R}\Big)\Big(1-1\wedge\frac{\|y\|_{X^{\alpha}}}{R}\Big).

Therefore, we have that

\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}[f_{R,\varepsilon}(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi),\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi^{\prime}))]dt=\iint f_{R,\varepsilon}(x,y)d\rho_{s}(x)d\rho_{s}(y)=2\gamma_{R,\varepsilon}.

(5.40)

Suppose that
\gamma_{R,\varepsilon}>0
. Since
f_{R,\varepsilon}\leq 1
and
f_{R,\varepsilon}(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi),\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi^{\prime}))=0
for
t<\tau
,
by (
5.40
) we can deduce that there exists
T({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})
such that

\mathbb{P}(\{\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})\leq T({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})\})\geq\gamma_{R,\varepsilon}.

Define recursively the stopping time
\tau_{k}
with
\tau_{0}=T({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})
, and

\tau_{k+1}=\tau_{k}+T(\Phi_{\tau_{k}}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi),\Phi_{\tau_{k}}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi^{\prime})).

We have that

\displaystyle\phantom{=\ }\mathbb{P}\{\tau>\tau_{k}\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\{\tau(\Phi_{\tau_{k-1}}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi),\Phi_{\tau_{k-1}}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi^{\prime}))>T(\Phi_{\tau_{k}}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi),\Phi_{\tau_{k-1}}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi^{\prime}))|\tau>\tau_{k-1}\}\mathbb{P}\{\tau>\tau_{k-1}\}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma_{R,\varepsilon})\mathbb{P}\{\tau>\tau_{k-1}\}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma_{R,\varepsilon})^{k+1}\to 0\text{ as }k\to\infty.

where we used the (strong) Markov property for the penultimate inequality, and a simple induction for the last inequality. Therefore,
\tau
is finite a.s., as long as
\gamma_{R,\varepsilon}>0
. We notice that

f_{R,\varepsilon}\geq\frac{1}{8}\quad\text{ for }\quad\|x-y\|_{X^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\,\|x\|_{X^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{R}{2},\,\|y\|_{X^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{R}{2}.

Let
R
be such that
\rho_{s}(B_{\frac{R}{4}}(\mathbf{0}))>0
, and let
x_{0}\in B_{\frac{R}{4}}(\mathbf{0})
be such that
\rho_{s}(B_{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}(x_{0}))>0
. Such an
x_{0}
must exist because countably many such balls cover
B_{\frac{R}{4}}(\mathbf{0})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\gamma_{R,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\iint f_{R,\varepsilon}(x,y)d\rho_{s}(x)d\rho_{s}(y)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{16}\iint\mathbf{1}_{\{\|x-y\|_{X^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\|x\|_{X^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{R}{2},\|y\|_{X^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{R}{2}\}}(x,y)d\rho_{s}(x)d\rho_{s}(y)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{16}\rho_{s}(B_{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}(x_{0}))^{2}>0.

We now move to proving (
1.14
). Fix
{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\in X^{\alpha}
, and let
F\in\Lip_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}
. By Proposition
5.10
and the (strong) Markov property, we have that

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\ }\int\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{0},\xi))\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi^{\prime}))\big]d\rho_{s}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbb{P}(\{\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})>t-t_{0}(n,R)\})d\rho_{s}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq}+\int\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})\leq t-t_{0}(n,R)\}}F(\Phi_{t-\tau}(\Phi_{\tau}({\mathbf{u}}_{0},\xi),\xi^{\prime\prime}))-F(\Phi_{t-\tau}(\Phi_{\tau}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi^{\prime}),\xi^{\prime\prime\prime}))\big]d\rho_{s}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{P}(\{\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})>t-t_{0}(n,R)\})d\rho_{s}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq}+\int\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})\leq t-t_{0}(n,R)\}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}[F(\Phi_{t-\tau}(\Phi_{\tau}({\mathbf{u}}_{0},\xi),\xi^{\prime\prime}))\big]

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq+\int\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})\leq t-t_{0}(n,R)\}}]\ }-\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{\prime\prime\prime}}[F(\Phi_{t-\tau}(\Phi_{\tau}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi^{\prime}),\xi^{\prime\prime\prime}))\Big)\Big]d\rho_{s}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbb{P}(\{\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})>t-t_{0}(n,R)\})d\rho_{s}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})+K^{\prime}(R)\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\xi^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime\prime}
are copies of space-time white noise (that correspond to
\xi(t-\tau),\xi^{\prime}(t-\tau)
respectively). Therefore, by Lemma
5.2
, and since
\tau
is finite a.s., by dominated convergence we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}^{\ast}\delta_{{\mathbf{u}}_{0}}-\rho_{s}\|_{d_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int\mathbb{P}(\{\tau({\mathbf{u}}_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})>t-t_{0}(n,R)\})d\rho_{s}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})+K^{\prime}(R)\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq K^{\prime}(R)\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, we deduce (
1.14
).
∎

Before proceeding to the proof of Theorem
5.10
, we need the following estimate.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
X\geq 0
be a random variable such that
\mathbb{E}[X]=1
. Then, for any
0\leq\theta\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|X-1\big|\big]\leq 2\big[1-\theta\,\mathbb{P}\big(X\geq\theta\big)\big]

Proof.

We write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|X-1\big|\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[(X-1)\mathbf{1}_{\{X>1\}}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[(1-X)\mathbf{1}_{\{X\leq 1\}}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[X\mathbf{1}_{\{X>1\}}\big]-\mathbb{P}(X>1)+\mathbb{E}\big[(1-X)\mathbf{1}_{\{X\leq 1\}}\big]

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{E}\big[X\mathbf{1}_{\{X\leq 1\}}\big]-\mathbb{P}(X>1)+\mathbb{E}\big[(1-X)\mathbf{1}_{\{X\leq 1\}}\big]

\displaystyle=2\big(1-\mathbb{E}\big[X\mathbf{1}_{\{X\leq 1\}}\big]-\mathbb{P}(X>1)\big).

Using that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[X\mathbf{1}_{\{X\leq 1\}}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\big[X\mathbf{1}_{\{X\geq\theta\}\cap\{X\leq 1\}}\big]\geq\theta\mathbb{P}\big(\{X\geq\theta\}\cap\{X\leq 1\}\big)

and that
0\leq\theta\leq 1
implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X>1)\geq\theta\mathbb{P}(X>1)=\theta\mathbb{P}\big(\{X>1\}\cap\{X\geq\theta\}\big),

we therefore have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|X-1\big|\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\big[1-\theta\mathbb{P}\big(\{X\geq\theta\}\cap\{X\leq 1\}\big)-\theta\mathbb{P}\big(\{X>1\}\cap\{X\geq\theta\}\big)\big]

\displaystyle=2\big[1-\theta\mathbb{P}\big(X\geq\theta\big)\big].

∎

Remark 5.12
.

We will make use of the following consequence of Lemma
5.11
: Let
1\leq p<\infty
, and let
X
be a random variable such that
\mathbb{E}[e^{X}]=1
and
\mathbb{E}[|X|^{p}]<+\infty
. Then, for any
L>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|e^{X}-1\big|\big]\leq 2\bigg(1-e^{-L}+e^{-L}L^{-p}\mathbb{E}\big[|X|^{p}\big]\bigg)

(5.41)

This follows from Lemma
5.11
by putting
\theta=e^{-L}
and applying Chebyshev’s inequality to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(e^{X}\geq e^{-L}\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\big(L\geq\log e^{X}\geq-L\big)=\mathbb{P}\big(|X|\leq L\big)=1-\mathbb{P}\big(|X|>L\big).

implies that the processes

are a.s. well-defined, where
{\mathbf{v}}_{j}(t;{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{0},\xi)
are the unique solutions to (
3.1
), for
j=1,2
, and moreover there exists
C_{1}=C_{1}(M,R)>0
such that

\displaystyle\max_{j=1,2}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{j}(t;{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{0},\xi)\|_{C([0,t\wedge\tau_{M}];\mathcal{H}^{1})}\leq C_{1}.

(5.42)

By Proposition
5.7
, we can define
{\mathbf{w}}\in C([0,\infty);\mathcal{H}^{1})
as the unique solution to (
5.17
) with
{\mathbf{w}}^{0}=0
and with respect to the stochastic flow
\Phi_{1}(t;{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi)
, and
{\mathbf{w}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{w}}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\lesssim e^{-ct}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}},

(5.43)

where the implicit constant is independent of
t,R,
and
M
.
From Lemma
5.8
, we form the function
h
according to (
5.34
) and denote by
h_{M}
the process
h
stopped at time
\tau_{M}
, that is,
h_{M}(t):=h(t\wedge\tau_{M})
. We have that
h_{M}
is adapted, and by (
5.36
) and (
5.42
), it satisfies

\displaystyle\|h_{M}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(M,R).

This implies that
h_{M}
satisfies the Novikov condition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|h_{M}(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dt^{\prime}\bigg)\bigg]\leq e^{tC^{2}_{2}(M,R)}<+\infty

for any
t>0
.
For
f\in L^{2}_{t,x}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(f):=\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|f(t^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dt^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{t}\langle f(t^{\prime}),\xi(t^{\prime})\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}dt^{\prime}\bigg).

By (
5.37
), we have that

\displaystyle\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi+h_{M})=\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi)+\mathbf{r}_{M}(t),

(5.44)

where

\displaystyle{\bf r}_{M}(t):=S(t\wedge\tau_{M})({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})+{\mathbf{w}}(t\wedge\tau_{M}).

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}\big\{F(\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}(\xi)}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0};\xi))-F(\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}(\xi)}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi))\big\}\big]

(5.45)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXX}+\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t>\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}\big\{F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0};\xi))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi))\big\}\big].

(5.46)

Since
F\in\text{Lip}_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle|\eqref{exp2}|\leq 2\|F\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{P}(t>\tau_{M}(\xi)).

(5.47)

Using (
5.44
), we have

(
5.45
)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}\big\{F(\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}(\xi)}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi+h_{M})-{\bf r}_{M}(t))-F(\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}(\xi)}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi+h_{M}))\big\}\big]

(5.48)

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}+\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}\big\{F(\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}(\xi)}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi+h_{M}))-F(\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}(\xi)}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi))\big\}\big].

(5.49)

Using (
5.1
), (
5.38
), (
2.4
) and (
5.43
), we have

\displaystyle|\eqref{exp3}|

\displaystyle\leq\|F\|_{d_{n}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}d_{n}(S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}),{\bf 0})\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}d_{n}({\mathbf{w}}(t),{\bf 0})\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\|F\|_{d_{n}}\big(1\wedge Cne^{-ct}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}\big).

(5.50)

Meanwhile, by Girsanov’s theorem,

(
5.49
)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}F(\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}(\xi)}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi+h_{M}))\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}F(\Phi_{t\wedge\tau_{M}(\xi)}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi))\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi+h_{M}))\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t\leq\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi))\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\big(F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi+h_{M}))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi))\big)\big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t>\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}\big(F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi+h_{M}))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi))\big)\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big(\mathcal{E}(h_{M})-1\big)\big]

(5.51)

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{t>\tau_{M}(\xi)\}}\big(F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi+h_{M}))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0};\xi))\big)\big]

(5.52)

We bound the second term by

\displaystyle|\eqref{exp6}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|F\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{P}(t>\tau_{M}(\xi)).

(5.53)

We now consider (
5.51
). For
L>0
to be chosen later, let
E:=\{\mathcal{E}(h_{M})\geq e^{-L}\}
. Since
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}(h_{M})]=1
, (
5.41
), Ito’s isometry and (
5.35
) imply

\displaystyle|\eqref{exp5}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|F\|_{L^{\infty}}\big[1-e^{-L}+e^{-L}L^{-1}\mathbb{E}[|\log\mathcal{E}(h_{M})|]\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\|F\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigg[1-e^{-L}+e^{-L}L^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|h\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}^{2}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\ \big]\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2\|F\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigg[1-(1-C(R)\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}L^{-1})e^{-L}\bigg]

(5.54)

Finally, combining (
5.47
), (
5.50
), (
5.53
) and (
5.54
), we have

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2\|F\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(1-(1-C(R)\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}L^{-1})e^{-L}\big)+4\|F\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{P}(t>\tau_{M}(\xi))

\displaystyle+2\|F\|_{d_{n}}\big(1\wedge Cne^{-ct}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}\big).

Using that
F\in\text{Lip}_{d_{n},\frac{1}{2}}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big]

(5.55)

\displaystyle\leq 1-(1-C(R)\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}L^{-1})e^{-L}+2\mathbb{P}(t>\tau_{M}(\xi))+2(1\wedge Cne^{-ct}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})

Now, by Chebyshev’s inequality and (
2.26
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(t>\tau_{M}(\xi))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max\big\{\|\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to4.0pt{\hss\hbox{

}\hss}}}}{}{}_{t}(\xi)\|_{C([0,t];\mathcal{W}^{\alpha,\frac{4}{\alpha}})}^{2},\|\hskip-2.0pt:\hskip-2.0pt{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{\hbox to4.0pt{\hss\hbox{

\hbox to3.4pt{\vbox to9.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.7pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}\hss}}}}{}{}_{t}(\xi)^{3}}\hskip-2.0pt:\hskip-2.0pt_{\gamma}\|_{C([0,t];L^{2}_{x})}^{2}\big\}\bigg]}{M^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(t)}{M^{2}}.

Inserting this bound into (
5.55
) yields

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\ }\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big]

\displaystyle\leq 1-(1-C(R)\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}L^{-1})e^{-L}+\frac{2C(t)}{M^{2}}+2(1\wedge Cne^{-ct}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}).

(5.56)

We first choose
L=L(R)\gg 1
, so that
2C(R)RL^{-1}<\frac{1}{4}
.
Next, we choose
t_{R}=t_{R}(n,R,L)=t_{R}(n,R)>0
so that

\displaystyle 2(1\wedge Cne^{-ct_{R}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})\leq 2(1\wedge 2CRne^{-ct_{R}})\leq\frac{1}{8}e^{-L}.

(5.57)

Then, we choose
M=M(R,t,L)\gg 1
so that

\displaystyle\frac{2C(t)}{M^{2}}<\frac{1}{2}e^{-L}.

Hence we have, for every
t\geq t_{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi))-F(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi))\big]

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{1}{4}e^{-L}+2\big(1\wedge Cne^{-ct}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}\big).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\eqref{tobesplit}\leq 1-\frac{1}{8}e^{-L(R)}=:1-K(R).

(5.58)

Let now
L=R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Then, let
t_{1}=t_{1}(C,n)
be such that

\displaystyle 2(1\wedge Cne^{-ct_{1}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}})\leq\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}\leq\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

and for
t\geq t_{1}
, let
M=M(R,t,{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0})\gg 1
so that

\displaystyle\frac{2C(t)}{M^{2}}\leq\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By our choice of
L
, we have that
L\leq\sqrt{2}
, so
e^{-L}\geq 1-L=1-R^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. We obtain that, for some constant
C^{\prime}(R)
,

\displaystyle\eqref{tobesplit}\leq C^{\prime}(R)\|{\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}\|_{X^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.59)

Therefore, (
5.39
) follows from (
5.58
) and (
5.59
), with
K^{\prime}(R)=\frac{C^{\prime}(R)}{1-K(R)}
.
∎

Remark 5.13
.

We would like provide some heuristic about our claim that the estimates in this paper can provide at best a sub-polynomial rate of convergence.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
. We want to estimate the first time
t
such that

\|\mathcal{P}_{t}^{\ast}\delta_{{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}}-\rho_{s}\|_{d_{1}}\lesssim\varepsilon.

Since the estimate (
5.39
) does not provide any information about what happens outside of a ball with radius
R
, we need to fix
R
with
\rho_{s}(B_{R}({\bf 0}))\lesssim\varepsilon
. In view of Proposition
3.5
, this requires us to take
R\gtrsim|\log\varepsilon|^{A}
, for some
A>1
.
We notice that by (
5.57
), we have

t_{0}\gtrsim L\gtrsim RC(R).

The quantity
C(R)
in this inequality comes from (
5.54
), which in turn depends on (
5.35
). From the proof of Proposition
5.8
, we see that the constant
C
in (
5.35
) satisfies

C\gtrsim\int_{0}^{+\infty}\eta(t,R)^{k(s,\alpha)}dt\gtrsim\exp(cR),

for some constant
c>0
, and a similar estimate holds for the quantity
C(R)
. Recalling that
R\gtrsim|\log\varepsilon|^{A}
, we obtain that

t_{0}\gtrsim\exp(c^{\prime}R)\gtrsim\exp(c^{\prime}|\log\varepsilon|^{A}),

which is a function of
\varepsilon^{-1}
with super-polynomial growth.

With
{\mathbf{w}}=0
and

\displaystyle h=\pi_{1}(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0};\xi)+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0}))^{3}-\pi_{1}(\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0};\xi))^{3},

we obtain

\displaystyle\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0},\xi+h)=\Phi_{t}({\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0},\xi)+S(t)({\mathbf{u}}_{2}^{0}-{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{0})

as the analogue of (
5.37
). This choice of

satisfies a corresponding estimate as in (

) since samples of the Gibbs measure

) under the flow of (

) then follows from the arguments in the proofs of Proposition
